R-D115 A product belongs to a list of plants
A part number is company-wide and may be made at one, several or all plants. product_sites keyed (product_id, node_id) records where; products.site_node_id is dropped. A product is readable when any of its plants is on the reader's branch, and product_sites rows only where the reader can read the node. A run or assignment is accepted where any assigned plant covers the cell (app_product_offered_at) and refused not_offered_here where none does. Operators, skills and shift patterns keep a single owner.
§19.81, D115 · maintainer · Only the backfill upgrade file is named; the read and offering rules themselves have no case named in §19.81.		 sql · supabase/tests/upgrade_0034_ (full name not given) backfill of one product_sites row per product covered
R-001 Each org defines its own hierarchy levels
An org configures an ordered list of level names of any depth. Exactly one level is flagged schedulable and its nodes become the rows of the board. Nothing in the schema hardcodes level names or depth.
§1, §2 · maintainer		 — uncovered
R-002 Only schedulable-level nodes accept runs and assignments
Runs and assignments may only be attached to nodes on the schedulable level.
§2, D72 · agent · The schedulable-level lock (R-D72) protects existing work; no test is named for refusing a write onto a non-schedulable node directly.		 — uncovered
R-003 Renaming a level is a metadata edit, never a migration
Levels can be renamed freely and added at the bottom without any schema change. Inserting a level mid-hierarchy is a guided tool, not a v1 feature.
§2 · agent		 — uncovered
R-004 Every tenant table carries org_id under row-level security
Every tenant-scoped table has an org_id column and RLS policies, so isolation holds even if application code drops a WHERE clause.
§3, §9 · agent		 sql · supabase/tests/40_rls_test.sql covered
R-005 Storage keeps exact timestamps; snapping is UI-only
Runs and assignments store precise instants as tstzrange. Fifteen-minute, hourly or shift snapping is purely a client setting and never touches the backend.
§1, §3, §10 · agent		 — uncovered
R-006 Board loads are one subtree-by-time-window query
Every board load asks only for runs and assignments under one subtree that intersect a time window. The UI never fetches all assignments and never refetches a whole schedule on change.
§3, §11 · agent		 — uncovered
R-007 A losing concurrent write gets a 409 and reverts
When two supervisors race for the same operator exactly one write wins. The loser receives a 409 with the conflict attached and the client reverts its optimistic block with a message naming the conflicting assignment.
§3, §5, §18.8 · agent		 manual: Force a second 100% assignment for a fully loaded operator; DevTools shows HTTP 409 with error=PT409 (§18.8) uncovered
R-008 Phase 1 builds no archive, partitioning, connector or template tables
The migrations must not create assignments_archive, partitions, integration_connections or Copy Week / template tables in Phase 1.
§3, §17 · agent		 — uncovered
R-009 Real-time events are per org-subtree channel, no polling
Row-level change events for runs and assignments are broadcast on channels scoped to an org and site subtree. A client subscribes only to the subtree it views and filters to its visible window. Presence rides the same channel. Nothing polls.
§5, §11 · agent		 — uncovered
R-010 Supabase is the v1 substrate with a designed escape hatch
v1 runs on Supabase (Postgres, RLS, Auth, Realtime). The design depends only on Postgres plus row-event broadcast so the realtime tier can be swapped later without redesign.
§5 · agent		 — uncovered
R-011 Skill requirements attach to any node and inherit downward
A cell's effective skill requirements are the union of requirements on every ancestor along its path. A requirement on a Line applies to every cell under it.
§6, D11 · agent · §17.2 reports the P1-3a suite caught a mutation of check_eligibility's ancestor containment, but does not name the test file.		 — uncovered
R-012 Eligibility checked in the UI and authoritatively at the API
When an operator is picked up, ineligible rows dim and expiring certifications warn, computed client-side. The API check on write is authoritative so no client bug can staff an uncertified operator.
§6, §10 · agent		 — uncovered
R-013 Certification expiry is checked against the assignment window
Expiry is tested against the assignment's time range, not today. Scheduling someone three weeks out fails if their certification lapses in two.
§6 · agent		 — uncovered
R-014 Eligibility policy is per org: warn (default) or block
An org chooses whether an ineligible placement is refused outright or allowed with a recorded override. The default is warn.
§6, §15.2 · agent		 — uncovered
R-015 Syncable tables carry source and external_id; CSV import upserts
Operators and products carry source and external_id. CSV import is an upsert keyed on (org_id, external_id) so re-importing an updated export just works, and it is the same pipeline a future connector uses.
§7, §19.36 · agent · §19.36 measured that nodes has no external_id column and products.external_id has no uniqueness constraint; the node import (P1-5h) needs its own migration first.		 — uncovered
R-016 Synced fields are read-only in-app; app-owned fields stay editable
Records that came from a sync lock their synced fields in the app. Skills and home node remain editable everywhere.
§7 · agent		 — uncovered
R-017 Deactivation is active=false, never a delete
Operators and products are deactivated, not deleted, so schedule history survives.
§7 · agent		 — uncovered
R-018 Copy Week always previews conflicts before committing
Copying a week forward is a bulk insert preceded by a mandatory dry-run that lists clean copies, conflicts and references to deactivated operators. The supervisor resolves or skips, then commits. Never a blind paste.
§8 · agent		 — uncovered
R-019 Named templates store relative placements
A template stores day-of-week plus start and end offsets with operator, product and node references, and stamps onto any future week through the same preview-then-commit flow.
§8 · agent		 — uncovered
R-020 Three roles: admin, supervisor, viewer
A profile has one role of admin, supervisor or viewer. Admins manage hierarchy, skills, integrations and users; supervisors edit schedules within granted subtrees; viewers are read-only.
§9, §14.3 · agent · superseded by R-D98 · D98 (§19.40) moves the role onto a (person, scope) pair so one person can hold several roles.		 — superseded
R-021 A grant is a node and means everything under its path
A subtree grant points at a node and scopes, via its ltree path, the rows a user sees, the roster they assign from and what RLS lets them write. Admins hold a root grant. This is enforced in RLS, not only in the UI.
§9, §14.3 · agent		 sql · supabase/tests/40_rls_test.sql covered
R-022 The board is an owned virtualized grid, not a chart library
The board is a React and TypeScript component that renders only visible rows and visible time, targeting smooth pan and zoom with hundreds of rows.
§10, D19 · agent		 — uncovered
R-023 Board layout: roster panel, hierarchy rail, rows, time header
A collapsible operator panel sits at the far left, then the hierarchy rail, then one row per schedulable node under a time-axis header. Collapsing a group collapses its rows.
§10, §15.3 · agent		 — uncovered
R-024 Snapping adapts to zoom; a modifier key drags free
Each zoom level has a default snap (shift at the coarsest, then 1 h, 15 min, 5 min). Holding a modifier key drags to the minute.
§10, §16.2, D16 · agent		 — uncovered
R-025 Board gestures: create, resize, move, click; all optimistic
Drag on empty row space creates; dragging block edges resizes; dragging a body moves it in time or across rows and re-checks eligibility live; click opens a details popover. Every mutation applies optimistically and rolls back on 409.
§10, §5, §18.13 · agent		 — uncovered
R-026 Every board supports both run and direct assignment creation
Drag-select can create a product run or a direct assignment at any time; a type selector in the creation popover chooses which.
§14.1 · agent		 — uncovered
R-027 The create-mode preference never changes how data renders
A profile's default_create_mode only pre-selects the popover's type. Two supervisors with different preferences see an identical board.
§14.1 · agent		 — uncovered
R-028 A run and a direct assignment may coexist on one cell
Direct assignments render as standalone operator blocks with a product chip; run-attached ones render as staffing chips under the band. A floater may help a cell mid-run.
§14.1 · agent		 — uncovered
R-029 Efficiency defaults to 100% and must be between 0 and 200%
assignments.efficiency defaults to 1.000 and is constrained to more than 0 and at most 2. The UI shows a badge on any block that is not 100%.
§14.2 · agent		 — uncovered
R-030 Effective headcount is the sum of efficiencies
A run's effective headcount is the sum of its staffing efficiencies, so two operators at 50% satisfy a planned headcount of 1. Understaffing is judged on expected output, not heads.
§14.2 · agent		 — uncovered
R-031 A target is a window total; unset renders NA
target_qty is a total for the assignment window, not a rate, with free-text units. Resizing an assignment that carries a target prompts keep-total or scale-proportionally.
§14.2 · maintainer · QUALIFIED BY R-316 (0040). "Unset renders NA" now reads "unset renders the standard the cell's cycle time implies, and NA only when there is no cycle time". target_qty itself is unchanged — still a window total, still the human's explicit override, still null when nothing was typed. The keep-total-or-scale prompt on resize is still unbuilt and still queued; note that it only ever applied to a TYPED target, and a derived one already follows a resize by being recomputed.		 — uncovered
R-032 The timeline is continuous and past edits are allowed and audited
The time axis scrolls across days in both directions. Editing the past is allowed in v1 and always audit-logged.
§14.4, §14.5 · agent		 — uncovered
R-033 An operator's instantaneous load may not exceed the capacity cap
At any instant the sum of an operator's non-cancelled assignment efficiencies must not exceed the org's cap (default 1.0). Split coverage such as 50/50 is legal; full double-booking is still refused.
§15.1, D2 · agent		 sql · supabase/tests/20_capacity_test.sql covered
R-034 Capacity trigger uses a per-operator lock and peak math
The check takes an advisory lock per operator so concurrent writes stay safe, and it evaluates the peak at the new range's start and at the starts of overlapping assignments, not a naive sum of overlapping rows.
§15.1 · agent		 sql · supabase/tests/20_capacity_test.sql covered
R-035 Exceeding capacity opens a split-coverage popover, not a dead end
A drop that would exceed capacity opens a popover listing the operator's overlapping assignments with editable efficiencies, a live peak indicator and a Split evenly action. Confirm stays disabled while the peak exceeds the cap; cancelling reverts.
§15.1, §18.13 · agent · §18.13: the split flow is reachable from a create or a panel drop but not from moving an existing chip, because apply_split_coverage cannot carry a timerange.		 — uncovered
R-036 Split evenly never spreads shares by more than one unit
An even split gives one extra unit to the first remainder participants so max share minus min share is at most 1 for any count and cap (three-way at 100% is 34/33/33).
§18.13 · agent · §18.13 says a property test caught the bug but does not name the test file.		 — uncovered
R-037 A run drags to another cell with its crew
A run moves to another cell like any block. The target must have no overlapping run, the crew moves with it, every crew member is re-checked for eligibility, and capacity is unaffected because times do not change.
§15.2, D4 · agent		 — uncovered
R-038 Roster entries show assignment count and dim when fully allocated
Each roster entry shows avatar, name, skill badges and a count pill with a tooltip listing the operator's assignments in the loaded window. Operators at full allocation render dimmed.
§15.3 · agent		 — uncovered
R-039 Shift templates attach to any node and resolve nearest-ancestor
A shift template is a named daily pattern of shifts, each with breaks. It attaches to any node and inherits downward; the nearest ancestor with a template wins.
§16.1, D12 · maintainer		 — uncovered
R-040 Shift times are minutes from midnight; overnight starts the day
start_min is 0–1439 and end_min may exceed 1440 to express an overnight shift, which belongs to the day it starts. Breaks use the same coordinates and must lie inside their shift.
§16.1 · maintainer		 — uncovered
R-041 Breaks render as a neutral hatched band; shifts as dashed boundaries
Breaks draw in the standard break colour beneath assignments with a tooltip naming the break. Shift boundaries draw as labelled dashed verticals and off-shift time gets a faint wash. Adjacent rows may legitimately show different stripes.
§16.2 · agent		 — uncovered
R-042 Breaks are display-only in v1
Breaks do not block assignments and are not subtracted from targets or capacity.
§16.2, §16.4 · agent · NARROWED BY R-316 (0040). Breaks ARE now subtracted from the DERIVED default target — that is the maintainer's own formula, "(how much time a person is assigned - breaks) / standard cycle time". Everything else in this row still holds: breaks do not block an assignment, are not subtracted from capacity, and are not subtracted from a target a human typed. The subtraction is a display computation over the cell's resolved shift pattern (standardTarget.ts), not a stored or enforced quantity.		 — uncovered
R-043 Compact zoom snaps to shift boundaries and offers full-shift quick actions
At Compact zoom drag-select snaps to shift boundaries and the creation popover offers one-click whole-shift creation for that row's pattern.

fitScale is the available height divided by the natural Standard height, clamped to 0.75–2.5, computed in one pass from unscaled heights. Every density field is rounded to an integer, NaN and Infinity are guarded, a chip always fits its lane and an avatar its chip.

R-075 place_node delegates guards to move_node and renumbers densely
place_node calls move_node for every structural guard, then renumbers the destination's children 0..n-1 so the node lands at p_index (clamped to 0..n, NULL means 0). Paths are untouched, the source parent is not renumbered, and repeating the call is idempotent.

§19.4, §19.36 · agent		 — uncovered
R-200 Three roles: system admin, site admin, supervisor/viewer
A system admin (org-wide user_profiles.role = 'admin') can affect anything at any site. A site admin is a profile_grants row with role admin on a site node and may change only that site. A supervisor holds a supervisor grant, may only do assignments and has no admin page. A viewer grant reads only. The role vocabulary is admin | supervisor | viewer.
§19.44 · maintainer		 sql · supabase/tests/46_scoped_roles_test.sql S1–S19 (S2 pins the role check constraint) covered
R-201 A person may hold several roles, one per place
A role is a property of a (person, place) pair, not of a person. Multiple roles are multiple profile_grants rows on different nodes; the key (profile_id, node_id) allows exactly one role per person per node. A supervisor on Line 3 who is also admin on Plant 2 is two grants.
§19.44 · maintainer		 sql · supabase/tests/46_scoped_roles_test.sql S5, S13, S15 covered
R-202 Grants add power and never subtract it
Coverage is the union of every grant whose node is an ancestor-or-self of the row, and the strongest role wins. Admin on Plant 1 plus viewer on Line 3 makes the person an admin on Line 3; giving someone read access to one line can never demote a plant admin.
§19.44 · agent		 sql · supabase/tests/46_scoped_roles_test.sql S7 covered
R-203 `app_is_admin_anywhere` is visibility only, never authorisation
app_is_admin_anywhere() answers only "should this person see the admin section at all". It never authorises a write.
§19.44, §19.47 · agent		 sql · supabase/tests/46_scoped_roles_test.sql S13, S14 covered
R-204 Node visibility follows grant paths; creators see their new row
nodes_select admits a row when the org matches and the caller is a system admin or the row's path is at-or-below one of the caller's grant paths. The predicate reads the row's own path column, never the nodes table, so an INSERT … RETURNING by a site admin or supervisor returns the created row instead of D85's silent empty result.

A company (system) admin reaches every site and may change anything anywhere. A site admin is locked to their site and may make any change that particular site needs without touching another site. R-214 Permission is checked after existence, not before
In template RPCs the ownership guard sits immediately after the org-scoped existence check, so a mistyped id in the caller's own org still answers invalid_argument / not found and only a real row the caller may not touch answers not_permitted. Cross-tenant callers learn nothing either way.

Creating a root, deleting or deactivating a root, moving a root, and moving a node to no parent require a company admin. R-226 A tree row offers peer slots or adopt, never both

R-227 Reordering among siblings is a legal drop
Dropping before/after a sibling that shares the dragged node's parent is a pure reorder and is accepted; the index handed to place_node is counted among the destination parent's children with the dragged node removed, taken from the rows the admin is looking at. R-228 No caret for a drop landing where the node already is
A placement whose destination parent is the node's current parent and whose index equals its current position offers nothing. In the tree the dead zone is dropped and the row's surviving placement takes the whole row; in the level list the dead half collapses into the live one, and the dragged row itself draws no caret.

R-247 A refused table write is reported as what it is
Plain table writes classify their failures: an RLS refusal is WriteRefused ("you don't have permission"), never "you need to sign in"; a unique violation is DuplicateValue; a foreign-key block is StillInUse; a check failure is InvalidValue; the shift exclusion constraint is ShiftOverlap while other exclusion constraints fall back to RaceLost. Every table write ends in .select() and requireWritten turns a zero-row UPDATE/DELETE into WriteRefused instead of "Saved".

R-248 Shift and break times: midnight, 24 hours and zero length
A shift or break may end exactly at minute 1440 (midnight) and counts as crossing midnight; a break may start after midnight (day marker on both ends, so {1440,1455} and {0,1455} render differently); a 24-hour shift is legal; a zero-length shift is refused; the overlap banner counts pairs.

R-256 A product colour is a palette token or one canonical hex

R-291 Product import matches on external_id, then sku
A row is judged in order: an external_id matching an existing part UPDATES sku and name unless the new sku already belongs to a different part; an unmatched external_id INSERTS; with no external_id, a matching sku UPDATES that part and a new sku INSERTS; two file rows sharing an external_id or a sku are both errors; an optional plant column assigns a product_sites row after the write, an unresolvable plant name is an error and a blank one is fine.

R-308 A site admin makes, and wholly owns, a product at their own plant (D116)
A site admin may add a part — the maintainer, 2 Sept: "a product is going to have unique part number, there is little to no risk to let a site admin add a new product." Because create is the birth of a company-wide identity, the whole lifecycle crosses together, bounded to a part the admin actually owns (labelled D116 in the code, migration 0036). CREATE: a site admin creates AT a plant they administer and the part is assigned to it in one transaction (the RPC create_product_at_node, gated on app_is_admin_for(node)); no company-wide orphan is left if the assignment is refused. RENAME, RECOLOUR, DELETE: allowed to a site admin only while EVERY plant that makes the part is one they administer (app_can_edit_product_record) — the moment a second plant adopts it, its identity is company property again. A company admin keeps the plant-less create and may change any record. This reopens the D115 Split, which had made the whole shared record company-only; the products UPDATE/DELETE policies and the delete_owned_row product branch widen from app_is_admin() to the shared predicate, and the client mirrors it (canEditProduct takes the row's makers and the reader's admin scope) as a fail-open hint with the server's WriteRefused as the backstop.

R-309 A system admin sets one date-display format for the whole company
A system admin picks one date-display format for the company and every calendar date the app shows as TEXT reads that way, while the data stays stored in its native ISO form. The maintainer, 3 Sept: "set the date format... the whole app should display dates in that format throughout." Settled org-wide (set by the system admin), not per-site: a plant is only a root node, and a per-site format would need the node-scoped nearest-ancestor resolution that per-site timezone (R-D88 / D88) reserves for Phase 2, which this does not touch. The choice is a CLOSED enum stored as a token in orgs.settings.date_format -- d_mon_yyyy (default; the shape the app shipped with), dmy_slash, mdy_slash, iso. TIMEZONE is out of scope by the same decision: the surfaces named show Postgres date columns, timezone-less by construction, so only the format changes what they read. Rendering goes through ONE seam (src/lib/format/dates.ts, formatCalendarDay) that is pure string work -- no Date, no Intl, no timezone, because parsing a date through new Date would move it a day west of Greenwich. The setting governs displayed text only: a native date input and CSV import parsing stay ISO. The write is an RPC (set_org_date_format, migration 0037) because a plain UPDATE is a silent zero-row no-op for a non-admin under orgs_update; it pre-checks app_is_admin, validates the token, and merges the one key so the rest of the settings bag survives. The Settings section is system-admin only, filtered on profile.role in AdminPage (adminSectionsFor returns "all" for a site admin too), with the RPC as the real gate. And so that a future date adopts the format automatically, dateSeam.test.ts fails the build when a date is formatted with Intl.DateTimeFormat, toLocaleDateString/toLocaleTimeString or a hand-rolled month array anywhere but the two allowlisted seams (dates.ts and board/lib/time.ts, plus operators.ts for the dependency-free default renderer).
the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session) · maintainer · Where it shows and where it cannot. WIRED: the board's day labels (range + column headers, weekday kept, format-only, still BOARD_ZONE) and the Operators expiry line. IMMOVABLE by nature, recorded not owed: a native `<input type="date">` (the board From picker, the Matrix/Operators certified/expires fields) is drawn by the browser in its own locale, and the certification import legend states the required ISO INPUT format for the CSV, which must stay deterministic. Board chip tooltips (formatFull) keep the default for now. First cut wired only the Operators popover line, which was invisible in normal use — the board labels were added the same day after the maintainer showed nothing had changed on screen. The closed enum is EIGHT presets as of 0038: d_mon_yyyy, dmy_slash, mdy_slash, iso, dmy_dash_mon (03-Sep-2026), d_month_yyyy (3 September 2026), month_d_yyyy (September 3, 2026), ymd_slash (2026/09/03).		 sql · supabase/tests/63_org_date_format_test.sql X1 (admin sets a format, capacity_cap/eligibility_policy survive the merge), X2 (the write persists), X3 (unknown token refused invalid_argument, nothing written), X4 (a supervisor refused not_permitted, setting untouched), X5 (null refused)

R-312 The training matrix header groups by plant when the plants are roots
The training-matrix column header groups each training under its full plant→area→line ancestry, with every plant appearing once, its columns contiguous, and PLANTS ORDERED ALPHABETICALLY (the maintainer's call). In this schema a plant IS a root (parentId null) and there is no company node above the plants, so a matrix that spans plants has NO common ancestor. buildColumns therefore must NOT trim to a single common root: lowestCommonAncestor returns "" when nothing is shared (a sentinel in no chain, so no column's path is cut and each keeps its plant). The column sort orders ROOT nodes by name (plants read A, B, C, D, not by a root sortOrder nobody arranges), while areas/lines/cells keep their deliberate sortOrder — which is the admin's drag order and also avoids a raw name sort putting "Line 10" before "Line 2" — with a name tiebreak so a level never falls through to the training name. A single-plant (scoped) view still has a real common ancestor and trims to it. The reported break: with four plants the header split one plant across two places (the old ?? ownerIds[0] fallback picked an arbitrary owner as the root) and floated an Area 2 column with no plant above it.

R-313 One shared pop-up shell, enforced, and the target unit no longer defaults to "units"
Every pop-up in the app renders through ONE shell, src/components/Popover.tsx (portal, role="dialog", aria-modal, focus trap, Escape, outside-pointerdown close, resolvePopoverPlacement, self-measurement), and a build audit forbids any other. Before this there was no standard: three parallel floating-panel implementations — the board's BoardPopover, a line-for-line clone AdminPopover, and a hand-rolled RecordPopover/place-chooser that lacked a focus trap and Escape. BoardPopover and AdminPopover are now thin aliases of the shared shell (their duplicated CSS deleted; AdminPopover.module.css left REM_SURFACES in both scaleAudit copies), and the matrix record popover and the Operators place-chooser compose it. popoverStandard.test.ts fails the build if role="dialog" appears outside the shared module — the shared-primitive-plus-audit pattern, as for icons and the date seam. The recurring "units" bug is fixed at its cause: the Target quantity+unit field was hand-duplicated in the create and edit popovers and both coerced targetUnit || "units", so a unit was written with no target. The field and its normaliser now live once in TargetField.tsx (normalizeTarget): no quantity means no unit (never the literal "units"), a unit is kept only beside a quantity (trimmed, 8-capped, blank -> null), and "units" is only a placeholder. Pop-up dates honor R-309: formatFull takes the org date-format token, threaded from BoardPage into the create/edit/run popovers.

R-314 The unit-needs-a-quantity rule holds for old data and is enforced by the database
R-313's client fix stopped NEW assignments getting a stray target_unit, but a code change only decides the next write — rows the old code already stored still carried a unit with no target_qty. The maintainer: "a rule is a rule, be it new or old." Migration 0039 makes it hold for every row and every future write: a BACKFILL nulls target_unit wherever target_qty is null, and a CHECK constraint (target_unit IS NULL OR target_qty IS NOT NULL) then refuses a unit without a quantity from ANY code path — old client, new client, an RPC, a hand-run UPDATE — so the invariant is a fact of the schema (the twin of target_qty's own check(>0)), not a convention the UI remembers. Data-transforming, so it carries an upgrade check over a pre-0039 database.
the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "a rule is a rule, be it new or old") · maintainer · Validated on a scratch DB built to 0038 (all four cases PASS) and applied to the dev DB — one dirty row backfilled to zero, a bad write refused. R-315 A standard cycle time may be set per schedulable node per product, in seconds
Migration 0040 adds node_product_cycle_times (node_id, product_id, seconds_per_unit numeric check(>0)), the standard seconds of work per one unit at one place. WHERE it may live is enforced by app_guard_cycle_time_scope, not remembered by the UI: the node's OWN level must be the schedulable one (asked off the node, never "the org's schedulable level" — since 0014 that index is per template, so an org with two structures has two right answers), and the product must be offered there by app_product_offered_at_in_org, the same predicate the run and assignment guards ask, so a cycle time can never exist where the part could not be scheduled. It is OPTIONAL everywhere and no write consults it. ALWAYS SECONDS: the admin grid offers seconds/minutes/hours and converts before writing, and displayCycle only offers a larger unit when the conversion is EXACT, so re-saving an untouched row cannot round 100 s into 1.7 min and back into 102 s. Reading is RLS-scoped downward (app_can_read_node); writing needs an admin grant on an ancestor-or-self (app_is_admin_for) with the company-admin escape. Both FKs cascade — delete_node deletes the two other per-node tables BY NAME and cannot know about this one, so without the cascade deleting any cell ever measured would fail with a foreign-key error.
the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "at each point in the lowest hierarchy where we actually do the assignment I want to assign a standard cycle time for each product which can be made in that hierarchy"), the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "this should not be mandatory... give the user ability to enter cycle time in seconds/minutes/hours but as a standard store it in the database in seconds so it is consistent") · maintainer · Applied to the dev DB and round-tripped there as the real company-admin persona through RLS, including the client's exact upsert path (create then replace). R-316 An assignment with no typed target shows what its cell's cycle time implies
An assignment carrying no explicit target_qty displays a DERIVED default: the minutes it covers, less the breaks of the shift pattern its cell resolves to, scaled by its efficiency, over the cell's seconds per unit. Whole units once one is achievable, one decimal below that — a 12 h cycle in an 8 h shift reads 0.7 rather than flooring to 0, because reporting nothing would read as impossible rather than as progress. NOTHING IS STORED: the value is recomputed on render, so a drag, a resize, a break edit or a re-pointed shift pattern is already right, and target_qty keeps its exact meaning as the human's override (R-031, R-313). No cycle time yields null, never zero, and the chip reads "target: NA" exactly as before. The derived value carries NO unit — units belong only beside a typed quantity, and inventing one would put back the bug R-313/R-314 exist to prevent. The two block shapes and both popovers share one reading and one computation, so a chip and the form that edits it cannot disagree.
the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "how much product can be made based on (how much time a person is assigned - breaks) / standard cycle time. This will become the default target for that assignment. This however can be overwritten by the already present mechanism"), the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "when no cycle time are defined, the target for the assignment is null") · maintainer · Qualifies R-031 ("unset renders NA" becomes "unset renders the standard, else NA") and narrows R-042: breaks are now subtracted from the DERIVED default. R-317 The level above shows the sum of the cells below, and it never derives a target
A line or plant row in the cycle-times grid shows the SUM of the measured cells at or below it for that part, computed on read and rendered as standard time per unit. That is the right reading as LABOUR CONTENT: two cells at 60 s and 90 s put 150 s of work into each unit. It is NOT the line's output rate — sequential stations ship a unit every 90 s and parallel ones every 36 s, neither of which is 150 — so the sum is display only and is never fed to the derived target, which always reads the cell's own number (R-316). Cells with no number contribute nothing rather than a zero (unmeasured work is unknown, not free) and the row reports how many contributed. Ancestry is the label-wise isAtOrBelow, so Line 10's cells never roll up into Line 1's total.
the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "my thinking is for the same product the standard time at a hierarchy 1 level above is the summation of standard cycle time in the level below") · maintainer · ⚠️ QUALIFIED BY R-319, and the maintainer's objection was sharper than the caveat written here. This row said a sum is right as LABOUR CONTENT even though it is not an output rate. That is true only where a unit passes through every child. For two parallel lines a unit goes down one or the other, so the sum is not labour content either — it describes nothing. A node now chooses, and the default only adds up where the children are the schedulable level. Everything else in this row still holds: where a row does add up, the number is labour content per unit, it is computed on read, and it never derives a target.		 vitest · src/test/cycleTimes.test.ts G2 a line shows the sum of its cells, G3 an unmeasured cell contributes nothing and the row says how many did, G4/G4b a part attached to one line is editable there only and a line is never typed into, G6 Line 10 is not inside Line 1 (a prefix test would merge them), G9 a summed row formats in the same friendly units, G10 the plant filter and the measured count
vitest · src/test/cycleTimesPanel.test.tsx the row half of the same clause, which had no test at all (DEF-0004): a place that does not make the part shows a dash named 'not made here', a line that makes it with nothing timed says '0 of 2 measured' in words rather than the same dash, the three states are three different accessible names, a partial sum is named as a count rather than as a fraction, a fully measured line shows the total alone, and the not-added row carries its counts in its name too		 covered
R-319 A node chooses whether it adds up its children's cycle times
R-317's roll-up added every measured place below a row, on the reading that a sum is the LABOUR CONTENT of one unit. That reading holds only where a unit passes through EVERY child. Two cells in a line are sequential stations, so a unit really does cost 60 s + 90 s; two lines under an area are ALTERNATIVE ROUTES, and no unit ever costs both, so adding them produced a number describing nothing — not a rate and not labour content either. Migration 0041 adds nodes.sums_children boolean, NULLABLE: true adds the places below up, false does not, and NULL means nobody has said. An unset node RESOLVES on read (resolveSumsChildren) rather than the column guessing: true only for a node whose own children are the schedulable level — a line over its cells, the one place summing is reliably right — and false for anything higher. Asked of a node's actual children, not of level positions, so an unevenly shaped tree answers by fact. A row that adds up totals every measured place BELOW it rather than composing its children's values, so one level declining cannot blank a row two levels up. A row that does not says "not added" with the counts behind it, never a bare dash. Display only: no target, capacity or write reads it (R-316 takes the cell's own cycle time), so a wrong setting misleads a reader and corrupts nothing. NOT a routing model — it cannot express one line feeding another while a third runs independently; that needs real routing data and stays the Phase 3 queue item. Editing it is a plain nodes UPDATE under the existing nodes_update policy, the same authority that renames a node.
the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "if two lines are working the same products but are parallel jobs, then adding them to the higher hierarchy does not make sense and is wrong... Some lines could be in continution of one another and some could be parallel to one another"), the maintainer, 3 Sept (in session, "I want to be able to choose to add or not to add") · maintainer · Driven in the browser as the company admin: lines defaulted to adding up and showed 30 min / 45 min / 15 min from their cells, the areas above them read "not added", and ticking Plant A turned it into 1.5 h over all six cells. The default was mine to choose — the maintainer left it — and it is stated plainly because it is a guess about plant shape, not a fact: cells within a line are almost always sequential, lines within an area almost never are. Every node is one click from the other answer.		 sql · supabase/tests/64_cycle_times_test.sql C11 a plant admin sets sums_children on their own line and it reads back, C12 the other plant's admin changes zero rows and raises nothing (the silent no-op, so nodes_update really does cover the new column), C13 the column starts unset so "nobody has said" stays distinguishable from "chosen false"
vitest · src/test/cycleTimes.test.ts G2 a line adds its cells and the plant above does not, G11a the resolved defaults per level, G11b the default is asked of a node's own children not its depth, G11c turning it on makes an area add its lines, G11d turning it off stops a line adding its cells, G11e a stored false is a choice and never collapses into unset, G11f a row with nothing measurable below stays "na", G11g resolveSumsChildren falsifiable on its own, G11h a row that adds up totals every place below rather than composing its children		 covered
R-320 The settings tab is one row per setting, not a screenful per setting
Settings shipped with the eight date formats as eight radio rows -- most of a pane spent on one preference, so the second setting to land would have been pushed below the first one's options. A setting is now ONE ROW: its name and hint on the left, one control on the right, and the next setting is another row rather than another screenful. The date format picks with a <select>, which is the right control for a closed enum of eight; a toggle or a number would sit in the same slot. THE SAMPLES CAME WITH IT -- the radio list showed a live sample of today's date beside every format, which is how a person picks one (by what they will actually see, not by a token's name), so each option reads "Day/Month/Year -- 03/09/2026" and the closed control therefore shows the sample for the current choice. The picker is the SHARED field (Field.module.css, R-318), not another copy of the skin, so this stylesheet came off that audit's legacy list in the same commit -- the list may only shrink, and this is the first entry to leave it. A site admin still SEES the setting, disabled with the reason in words, and the client refuses the write itself rather than leaning on disabled alone; the RPC (set_org_date_format, 0037) refuses them regardless.

R-318 One shared editable box, enforced — a field looks and behaves the same everywhere
The cycle-times grid shipped with browser-default controls because the app's field look was never in one place: the same block (1px --axis border, 0.375rem radius, --page background, 0.8125rem) was hand-copied into eleven admin stylesheets, so a twelfth screen had nothing to import and silently got the user agent's own. This is R-313's shape one layer down — there it was the pop-up SHELL, here it is the field inside it. The fix is the same: ONE shared module. src/components/Field.module.css is now the single definition of the box, the select and the button, and src/components/InlineEdit.tsx is the single edit-in-place behaviour — a resting box that LOOKS editable rather than a dash (so an empty cell reads as somewhere to type, not as missing data), and an open editor that can always be left without saving, by a visible Cancel as well as Escape. Every editable cell keeps the same box geometry as its neighbours, so a row of them does not appear indented against the read-only cells beside it. An audit stops a thirteenth hand-rolled field: the stylesheets that predate the standard are listed as legacy, and a file not on that list which declares its own field border is a build failure.

R-324 No run carries a status either — and the overlap rule stops making an exception for one
runs.status is gone. It was write-once 'planned' — nothing in the product has ever advanced a run to active, done or cancelled, which migration 0029 recorded as fact and worked around by using the clock — and runs have always been hard-deleted, so 'cancelled' was a state no run could reach. ⚠️ THE REASON THIS WAS SPLIT OFF FROM R-323 IS runs_no_overlap_on_node, the rule that stops two runs sharing a cell and a window. It is not a function that can be edited: it is a constraint on the table, and its definition carries where (status <> 'cancelled'). Removing the column means DROPPING that constraint and adding it back without the exception — the protection is off in between, and a replacement written even slightly wrong is silently weaker rather than loudly broken. So the new constraint is asserted from the outside afterwards: an overlapping run must be REFUSED, on the same cell, including the case that used to be the exception. ⚠️ AND THE EXCEPTION IS NOW A REAL REFUSAL. 10_constraints_test.sql case 7c asserted that "an overlapping run with status=cancelled is accepted". That was true and is now impossible to express; the case is inverted rather than deleted, because the change of contract is the point. Any cancelled run is deleted before the constraint is rebuilt, with its assignments first — not housekeeping but a precondition, since an unconditional exclusion constraint cannot be created at all over rows that violate it, and a cancelled run overlapping a live one is exactly the shape the old exception permitted.
the maintainer, 4 Sept (in session, after the assignments half landed and the run half was explained and queued, "Let's go ahead and remove it from runs as well") · maintainer		 sql · supabase/tests/67_no_run_status_test.sql X1 the column is gone and the constraint is no longer partial; X2 an overlapping run on the same cell is REFUSED; X3 the case that used to be ALLOWED (the cancelled-run exemption) is refused now, which is the whole change; X4 the same window on another cell is still accepted and X5 an adjacent window on the same cell is still accepted, because a rule that refused everything would pass X2 and X3 and be worse than the one it replaced; X6 create_run and move_run work with no status to write, and neither payload carries the key
sql · supabase/tests/10_constraints_test.sql case 7c, INVERTED: it asserted that an overlapping run with status=cancelled is accepted, which was true until 0044 and is now impossible to even express
vitest · src/test/boardIndex.test.ts every run in the window reaches a map — the positive form of the deleted case 17, since 'nothing is filtered' cannot be seen by removing an assertion		 covered
R-323 Deleting an assignment deletes it — assignments carry no status at all
Deleting an assignment removes the row. There is no status column on assignments and no soft delete: the same act that already removes a run's assignments when the run is deleted is now what the Delete button on a single assignment does. The audit log keeps the record — a trigger has written actor, action and the whole before/after row for assignments since migration 0007, and it is live (52 inserts, 5 updates, 12 deletes in the maintainer's own database), so "who removed this and when" was never what the status column answered. ⚠️ WHY THIS IS A DELETION AND NOT A RENAME. R-322 removed the picker that set planned / active / done and narrowed the write type to the literal "cancelled", which left one bit — live or gone — spelled as a word in a text column. Two things then made the column indefensible rather than merely redundant. The same table already deleted BOTH ways: delete_run hard-deletes a run's assignments while the assignment's own Delete marked them cancelled, so one concept had two behaviours depending on which button you pressed. And migration 0029 had already written down that the status vocabulary cannot be trusted — "nothing in the product ever advances it: every run this system has created is still 'planned', including the ones that finished last week" — and deliberately used the clock instead. The column was being routed around rather than used. ⚠️ THE DANGEROUS PART IS THE PREDICATES, NOT THE COLUMN. Four functions filtered on it and must be redefined from their LAST definitions before it can be dropped: operator_peak_load and capacity_probe (an operator's load must not count a cancelled row), check_operator_capacity (which returned early for a cancelled row), and move_run (a run's crew). With no cancellable row left, each clause is trivially true and goes; what must be proved is that the two guards still refuse what they refused before — a double-booked operator and an overlapping run — because those are the two rules that stop a person or a cell being committed twice. ⚠️ runs.status IS OUT OF SCOPE HERE AND STAYS, deliberately. Runs are already hard-deleted, so their status is write-once 'planned' and its only reader is an exclusion constraint clause that can never fire. Removing it means dropping and recreating that constraint, which is the one piece of this that touches a correctness invariant nobody asked about; it is queued, not smuggled in.
the maintainer, 4 Sept (in session, "why do we need this in runs or assignments? We removed the option to assign status, what am I missing here?" and, on being offered gone versus kept-and-hidden, "I want it gone") · maintainer		 sql · supabase/tests/66_no_assignment_status_test.sql X1 the column and the trigger clause that named it are both gone; X2 the capacity guard still REFUSES a second full booking of the same operator in the same window; X3 the overlap rule still refuses a second run on the same cell; X4 a delete removes the row AND the audit log records it with its actor; X5 an RLS-filtered delete outside a supervisor's grant removes zero rows and raises nothing, which is why the client checks ROW_COUNT; X6 deleting a run still takes its assignments, the behaviour this one now matches
vitest · src/test/dragGesture.test.ts R-322/R-323: saving an assignment sends no status, and Delete calls deleteAssignment rather than writing a field edit
vitest · src/test/boardIndex.test.ts a cancelled RUN is still excluded from every map; and every assignment in the window now reaches a map — asserted positively, because 'nothing is filtered' cannot be seen by deleting an assertion		 covered
R-322 An assignment has no progress label — only the soft delete that actually does something
The assignment pop-up offers no status control. It used to offer planned / active / done, and this file's own header had already flagged it as "a brief-only addition — neither openChipPop nor openDirectPop has a status control in the mockup". Nothing read the value: no rule fired on it, no screen displayed it, and nothing obliged a supervisor to set it. A field that is optional, invisible and consequence-free does not stay accurate — it sits at "planned" on work that finished last week — and a label nobody maintains is worse than no label, because a reader takes it for fact. ⚠️ cancelled IS UNTOUCHED AND IS NOT A LABEL. It is the soft delete: it frees the cell's slot past runs_no_overlap_on_node, returns the operator's hours to the capacity guard, and drops the row from every board map (boardIndex.ts rule 17) while keeping it in history for the deleted-product path. It is written by Delete, was never offered in the picker, and reaches the database through the same AssignmentFieldEdit.status field — which is now typed as the literal "cancelled" rather than string, so a progress label cannot be typed back in without failing to compile. The COLUMN stays as it is, with its default and its four permitted values, because history holds rows that carry them and the runs table uses the same vocabulary. What is removed is the control, the save path that carried it, and the open string type that invited it back. A future "the job actually started" is a shop-floor feedback problem — a scan, a line signal — not a dropdown a supervisor is trusted to remember, and it would be designed then.
the maintainer, 4 Sept (in session, "No one is going to use the status option to change it to active in practice, it is added work with no returns, the supervisor will be like I'll just delete the assignment or modify it if there is a change. It doesn't serve a purpose plus there is nothing making it mandatory for a supervisor to do") · maintainer		 vitest · src/test/dragGesture.test.ts R-322: saving an assignment sends no status at all, and Delete still writes cancelled — the half tsc cannot see, since the type stops a status being TYPED but says nothing about whether the save path still SENDS one covered

Session 62 · 2026-09-04 · Settings rows line up, and a rule can be answered for one plant counts confirmed

Two maintainer observations on the Settings screen, both of which turned out to be structural rather than cosmetic. ⭐ THE PICKERS DID NOT LINE UP BECAUSE THE ROW ASKED EACH ONE HOW WIDE IT WANTED TO BE. The control column was flex: 0 0 auto, which is literally "size me by my own longest option", and the eligibility labels are far longer than the date ones — so two rows could only align by coincidence. SettingRow.module.css is a second shared module beside Field.module.css: that one owns the BOX, this owns the ROW. A grid, because a track is a constant shared by every row rather than a negotiation with whatever text is in one; minmax(0, 1fr) and not 1fr, because a track's automatic minimum is its content width and a long hint would shove the column off the shared edge — the same bug in a different unit. The panel's own max-width: 20rem on its consequence paragraph turned out to be a second copy of the column width kept in step by hand, and existed only because of the flex bug. ⚠️ THE AUDIT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT MATCH ON GEOMETRY. display: flex plus justify-content also builds toolbars and card headers, so matching those would flag half the repo; it matches a stylesheet that NAMES a rule after a setting and then lays it out, plus a token declared exactly once, plus a count in the panel. No legacy exemption list, which is the difference from R-318: the field skin already had thirty copies so naming them was the only honest option, whereas the row existed in one file and a list would be a hole dug in advance. ⛔ THE PER-PLANT WORK TURNED ON ONE DECISION AND IT WAS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE. Storage is a TABLE, not a jsonb bag on nodes, because in a bag "key missing" and "key holds null" read back identically and F-088's CHECK cannot tell them apart. A row means this place was given an answer; no row means it inherits; value is NOT NULL, which closes F-088's hole at the column; and clearing is a DELETE rather than a magic value, with two verbs so that set(…, null) can never put the ambiguity back. ⭐ AND THE RESOLVER HAD TO BE SECURITY DEFINER, WHICH A COMPANY-ADMIN TEST CANNOT SHOW. nodes is RLS-scoped and move_run is INVOKER, so a supervisor granted one LINE cannot read the plant root the override sits on — an invoker walk would miss it and fall through to the company default, quietly handing a strict plant a permissive answer. The lane's first draft ran every case as the company admin, who can read everything, and the wrong version passed 16 of 16. Two cases driving a supervisor are what fail. That is the demo-world lesson from session 56 in a second place: a test that cannot fail for the reason it names. Resolution is nearest ancestor-or-self, then the org bag, then the coded default. Per NODE rather than per plant is both cheaper and more general — a rule that stopped at plants would have to know which level is a plant, and since 0014 that is a per-template question with no single answer. ⚠️ THE SCREEN'S PREDICATE WAS THE OTHER TRAP. node_settings is written under app_is_admin() OR app_is_admin_for(node) — arms one and two. app_can_edit_node carries a THIRD arm (app_can_write() plus a writable grant) that an org-wide supervisor with a supervisor grant satisfies and that the write policy refuses. Using the familiar canEditNode would have offered a control the server rejects. Both bodies were read from the live database rather than argued about. A plant somebody cannot administer is LISTED with no control and a reason, not greyed (a disabled control is named after something it does not do) and not dropped (silently missing is the invisible, permanent failure). ⚠️ TWO CORRECTIONS, ONE OF THEM MINE. I briefed that apply_split_coverage inherits eligibility by calling check_eligibility; the lane reported it consults eligibility NOT AT ALL and filed that as a gap; neither was right. It writes its row by calling create_assignment, which calls check_eligibility — enforced one hop further along than I said, and no gap. Read out of the function body rather than settled by argument, and nothing filed. Separately, the panel's own header claimed the section is system-admin-only and that AdminPage hides it from a site admin; adminSectionsFor returns "all" for anybody with adminAnywhere, which every site admin is, so the comment was false and R-331 rests on it. OWED: the board still applies one company-wide value to every plant, so a plant set to refuse would offer an override tick the server then refuses — F-087's shape a third time. ⛔ The obvious fix is wrong and was measured to be: resolving in the browser FAILS OPEN, because board_window is INVOKER and a supervisor never receives the plant root's override. It must be server-resolved. Also still open: the write gate was widened so a SITE admin may set their own plant's rule, which hands a safety rule to more people and is the maintainer's to confirm.

Session 61 · 2026-09-04 · The Activity screen answers who, what and when — three lanes and an integration counts confirmed

Four things the maintainer asked for after using the screen, built in three parallel lanes on disjoint files and wired together afterwards. 1869 in 67 files to 1904. ⛔ THE GATE ON 0046 IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THE COMMIT, because audit_actor_identities() is a door onto everybody in the company. It refuses a non-company-admin and RAISES rather than returning empty, since an empty table is what a broken function returns; the org join is the only thing between one company's admin and another's address book; and it takes no arguments, so there is no id to widen. ⭐ THE LANE PROVED ITS OWN TEST COULD CATCH A LEAK by breaking the function five ways and measuring each — no gate and no filter fails five cases, either alone fails two or three, and folding the gate into the WHERE so a site admin gets silence instead of a refusal fails two. That is the right standard for a security boundary and it was not asked for. Both headline claims were then re-checked from this session against the running database: company admin 6 rows, site admin refused. ⭐ THE FILTER PROBLEM WAS THE INTERESTING ONE. The list is keyset-paged, so a filter applied to the loaded page answers a question nobody asked: "removals, last 7 days" would show whichever removals happened to be in the most recent fifty rows and an empty list otherwise, under a footer confidently stating a count. The answer is that a NOW-ANCHORED period is a PREFIX of a newest-first read, so one row older than the cutoff proves nothing unread can match — the screen can therefore complete such a period by itself and say so truthfully. Periods that do not end at now cannot, and are not offered; that is written into the file as a decision rather than left as a gap. The unfiltered footer is word-for-word what it always said. ⚠️ THE ANSWER TO "WHO" IS AN EMAIL ADDRESS AND THAT IS A LIMITATION, NOT A DESIGN. user_profiles has nowhere to put a name, so the address is the only identity this system holds. The map returns an OBJECT rather than a string precisely so a display_name later is one more expression in the migration and no change to any caller. Names replaced ids in the Change column, and the rule there is that the SNAPSHOT wins over a live lookup — which is what keeps a deleted row nameable and stops a rename rewriting the past. Measured on real rows: the denormalised name columns are null on every live row except on the delete path, so the snapshot answers precisely the deletes and the lookups answer everything else. A run gets no invented name, because a run has none; Run · 000005 rather than "Unknown run", since reporting a failed lookup where there is nothing to look up is its own small lie. Deletions read as a coloured left accent on EVERY row rather than a red border on some, so the width is constant and the reader learns one system instead of one exception, and the word stays beside it so colour never carries the meaning alone. On a delete the per-field strikethrough goes: that row is not the discarded half of a pair, it is the whole of what was lost. fetchAuditActors was deleted in the same commit as the screen that called it, being strictly the poorer half of the newer read — the renameSkill trap from session 57, not re-set. The address case was verified by removing the wiring and watching it go red. ⚠️ TWO THINGS FOUND ON THE WAY, BOTH FILED. db:types cannot see nullability through a RETURNS TABLE, so it emits email: string for a column that is nullable and tsc would accept .toLowerCase() on a null. And seed.sql spells org 1's three addresses TWICE — inserted as @northwind.example, rewritten to @example.test by the GoTrue block about 180 lines later — which turned a hardcoded expectation red and is now read from the database instead. OWED AND NAMED: pushing the filters into the query itself (.gte("at", …), .in("action", …)) would make a page mean fifty MATCHES rather than fifty rows scanned, and would let a bounded period exist at all. The client half degrades honestly without it and would simplify, not change shape.

Session 60 · 2026-09-04 · Two lanes off the cleaned queue — retiring a shift pattern, and reading the audit log counts confirmed

Two parallel lanes, exclusive files, each writing a failing test first, each reviewed by somebody other than its author. 1800 in 62 files to 1869 in 67. ⭐ THE SHIFT LANE'S REAL WORK WAS DECIDING WHAT RETIRING DOES NOT DO. shift_templates.active has existed since 0029 with nothing able to set it. Measured against the running database: the company admin sees 225 rows, the site admin sees zero. Keyset-paged on id < cursor, never .range(), because the top of this list moves while it is read and offset paging repeats a row on one page and skips one on the next. A subject's name comes out of the snapshot and never a join — after a delete the row is gone, and after a rename the snapshot holds the name as it was at the time, which is the only correct answer. ⚠️ THE TIMESTAMP TRAP, CAUGHT AND THEN CHECKED AGAINST REAL ROWS. timerange is decoded from its raw range literal, and a Postgres timestamp is NOT an ISO string — a space instead of T, a two-digit offset instead of +00:00 — which new Date() accepts only by implementation grace and would otherwise have rendered NaN down the busiest column. The regex was written from an example, so it was run against actual audit_log rows from the dev database rather than trusted. The lane also caught a bug in its own work: showLoading was enabled && log.isPending, and with enabled: false React Query v5 leaves the companion flag false, so while the session resolved the screen said "Nothing has been changed yet" — a claim about the company made before a row had been requested. D91 for the third time, now with a case. ⚠️ A DECISION LEFT FOR THE MAINTAINER, NOT INVENTED. Naming an actor is impossible from the client: audit_log.actor_id is auth.uid(), user_profiles holds no name and no address, the email lives in auth.users which PostgREST does not expose, and site_people is keyed by node and returns a profile id. So "Who" reads You, System, or Supervisor · 0000b2 — the role plus a short tail — and never invents a name. Closing it wants a SECURITY DEFINER function mapping uid to an identity, gated on app_is_admin(), plus a decision about showing addresses; and if names are wanted rather than emails, there is nowhere to put one today.

Session 59 · 2026-09-04 · A certification cannot outlive the plant it was earned in (R-326, migration 0045) counts confirmed

"I do not want cross-plant certifications." Written into requirements as R-326 before any code, which is what a decision made in conversation is owed. Most of the rule already existed: 0028's app_guard_operator_skill_scope refuses to CREATE a holder row whose training and person sit on different branches. What was missing was the other half of the same sentence -- the invariant was checked when the pairing was MADE and never again when either END moved. So the state the insert guard forbids was reachable sideways, by moving the training or the person afterwards. 0045 gives both rehome guards the check. ⛔ THE ONLY SUBTLE THING IN IT, AND IT WOULD HAVE BEEN AN EASY WRONG ANSWER. The two rehome guards already had app_owner_covers_in_org, which is ONE-directional. The grant guard asks a DIFFERENT question -- containment in EITHER direction -- and that is deliberate: a person owned by a whole plant may legitimately hold a training owned by one of that plant's lines. Reusing the narrower helper would have refused moves that leave a pairing the app is perfectly happy to grant, which is a server refusing what the server allows. The predicate is extracted into ONE function, app_owner_overlaps_in_org, and called from all three sites, so the guards agree by construction rather than by everyone remembering. C3 is the case that proves it: it fails against the old helper and passes against the new one. ⭐ THE TEST'S FIRST RUN WAS RED FOR THE WRONG REASON AND THAT WAS THE USEFUL PART. Six of eight cases failed, none of them because of 0045. Two guards standing in front of the one under test, returning confident red. Rewritten onto purpose-built subjects that own nothing and are owed nothing. C6 was worse: it had reached for an ORG 2 training, where the composite FK (org_id, skill_id) refuses the insert long before any trigger runs -- a green case pinning nothing. It now uses a Plant 2 training in the same org. ⚠️ NO BACKFILL, AND THAT IS A CHOICE RATHER THAN AN OMISSION. These are triggers, not a constraint, so unlike 0044 nothing had to be deleted to install them. There are no offending rows here; on a database that has some, quietly deleting somebody's certification history to tidy a guard would be the wrong trade, since the rows are invisible rather than dangerous. WARN⚠️ A HAZARD FOUND ON THE WAY, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS ENTRY. supabase migration list --local reports migrations 0037 THROUGH 0045 with an empty remote column -- the local ledger does not record them as applied -- while the schema plainly HAS them (0044's dropped column is exactly why the demo world broke). They were applied by hand. The consequence is live: supabase migration up would try to re-run all nine, including 0044's DELETE FROM runs WHERE status <> 'planned' against a column that no longer exists. It was not run. 0045 was applied the way the others evidently were, by psql -f, and db:types regenerated (4 insertions, exactly app_owner_overlaps_in_org) so the drift closed in session 58 stayed closed. Session 54 · 2026-09-04 · No run carries a status either, and the overlap rule loses its exception (R-324) counts confirmed

The other half, taken deliberately rather than smuggled in with the first: "Let's go ahead and remove it from runs as well." runs.status was write-once 'planned' — 0029 had already established that nothing ever advanced a run and used the clock instead — and runs have always been hard-deleted, so 'cancelled' was a state no run could reach and the exception that read it could never fire. ⭐ THE EXCEPTION IS WHY THIS WAS ITS OWN PIECE OF WORK. runs_no_overlap_on_node is not a function that can be edited in place; it is a constraint on the table, and its definition carried where (status <> 'cancelled'). Removing the column means DROPPING the rule that stops two jobs sharing a cell and adding it back — and a replacement written slightly wrong raises nothing at all, it just starts accepting overlaps. So 67_no_run_status_test.sql asks the rule rather than reading it: an overlap is refused, the same window on ANOTHER cell is still accepted, and an ADJACENT window on the same cell is still accepted. Without those last two, a constraint that refused everything would pass. ⚠️ AND ONE OF THEM CAUGHT MY OWN TEST BEFORE IT COULD PASS FOR THE WRONG REASON. The adjacency case first hung a run off the end of a SEEDED one and was refused — because the seed puts consecutive shifts on the same cell, so "just after this ends" was already occupied. The constraint was right and the case was wrong. It builds its own pair now, in a window nothing else touches. Case 7c in 10_constraints_test.sql asserted "an overlapping run with status=cancelled is accepted" and was right until today; it is INVERTED rather than deleted, because the contract changing is the deliverable and a quietly missing case would hide it. Two other SQL files listed the column in their run inserts and no longer do. On the client, boardIndex now filters nothing at all — both status lines are gone, replaced by positive cases asserting every run and every assignment reaches a map. Suite 1788 in 60 files, tsc 0 with db:types regenerated, every SQL file green. Driven in the browser: the board draws its runs and a run's pop-up still opens, edits and deletes.

Session 52 · 2026-09-04 · The assignment status picker is removed (R-322) counts confirmed

The maintainer, on the planned / active / done picker in the assignment pop-up: "No one is going to use the status option to change it to active in practice ... That is worse than an absent field: a reader takes a label for fact. Deleting the field outright would have taken Delete with it. So the field stays and its TYPE narrowed from string to the literal "cancelled", which says what it is for and makes a progress label fail to compile rather than reach the database and go stale there. The pin is at the seam rather than in the pop-up, because tsc covers only half of this: the type stops a status being TYPED into a field edit, and nothing in the type system says the save path stopped SENDING one or that Delete still does. The maintainer asked why the workflows were still failing when we had fixed them. A supervisor granted one line opened her own board, saw four parts on her legend, was offered ONE, and the server accepted all four. DEF-0002 was a control SHOWN for something the server refuses — loud, because every attempt ends in a 409. This was a control HIDDEN for something the server permits, which says nothing at all: three quarters of her work simply could not be scheduled and no screen explained why. THE CAUSE WAS NOT THE RULE BUT ITS MATERIAL, and that distinction is the lesson. product_sites is RLS-filtered on read and reading is downward from a grant, so a part made at the PLANT above her had its only place row dropped before it reached her client. Every version of the client predicate then answered "offered nowhere" — correctly, for the list it was handed. Two states arrived as the same empty array: "assigned to no plant" and "every place is above your grant". Nothing downstream could tell them apart, because the information was gone before it got there. It was invisible to every role that could report it, too: an admin's grant covers everything their own board can show. So the client stopped deriving the answer and started being told it. Migration 0042 adds app_offered_product_nodes — the set form of app_product_offered_at, SECURITY DEFINER for the same reason that one is — and board_window carries offered_node_ids per product: the nodes in THIS window where a run would be ACCEPTED. The picker is membership and nothing else; no path logic is left on the client that can disagree with the server. The other option, widening product_sites_select, was not taken: it changes a read policy and hands a line supervisor the existence of a plant she has no grant on, where answering the question changes no policy and discloses strictly less. The DEFINER helper therefore filters its own output, and X4–X6 are the cases that would catch that slipping — a root above her grant, a sibling department, and the other tenant, which uses the same paths. ⚠️ THE TESTER'S SECOND FINDING IS THE ONE WORTH KEEPING. DEF-0002's fix argued in scope.ts that "nothing legitimately offerable disappears" — sound about the change it made, and then generalised to the picker as a whole, which it never examined. Case XP9 was GREEN while asserting the opposite of what the server answers, and my review of that lane accepted both the argument and the case. They are gone with the path logic they described. F-080's rule, "ask what narrowed the map", now carries its limit: it can only be applied by someone who knows what narrowed it, and here both had happened, one layer apart. When a question turns out to need information the client was never given, move the question rather than sharpening the guess. DEF-0002's pin was promoted in the same commit, as CLAUDE.md allows now that it is verified: its claim survives as XP4 beside the new predicate, and its pin field points at the new home. Checked in the browser as Ana — four parts offered where one was — and the admin's board still lists exactly its own plant's four, so DEF-0002 has not come back. 1582 app tests in 44 files · tsc 0 · eslint 0

Session 40 · 2026-09-03 · A node chooses whether it adds its children up (R-319) counts confirmed

The maintainer, looking at the roll-up: "if two lines are working the same products but are parallel jobs, then adding them to the higher hierarchy does not make sense and is wrong. Some lines could be in continuation of one another and some could be parallel to one another." This is a sharper objection than the caveat R-317 already carried. That caveat said a sum is not an output RATE but is still labour content per unit. True only where a unit passes through every child: two cells in a line are sequential stations, so a unit really does cost both, but two lines under an area are alternative routes and no unit ever costs both — so for parallel lines the sum is not labour content either, it describes nothing. Offered three designs, the maintainer cut it short: "I want to be able to choose to add or not to add." So it is one nullable boolean per node and no more. NULL means nobody has said and resolves on read rather than the column guessing, which keeps a chosen "false" distinguishable from an unset one and leaves nodes created later working without touching create_node. The default was mine to pick since they left it: add up only where a node's own children are the schedulable level, asked of the actual children rather than of level depths, which is right for a line over its cells and wrong nowhere it is not one click from fixing. A row that adds up totals every measured place below it rather than composing its children's values, so one level declining cannot blank a row two levels above. A row that does not says "not added" with the counts behind it. No new RLS: the existing nodes_update policy already covers every column, which C11-C13 are what actually establish rather than assume. Display only throughout — no target reads it, so a wrong setting misleads a reader and corrupts nothing, which is why a plain toggle is proportionate and a routing model is not. Driven in the browser: lines defaulted to adding and showed 30/45/15 min, the areas above read "not added", ticking Plant A totalled 1.5 h over six cells. Suite 1746 in 56 files, up from 1738.

1746 app tests in 56 files · tsc 0 · eslint 0 · commits cef0c6b

Session 39 · 2026-09-03 · A target computed from the standard is not the standard (F-077) counts confirmed

The maintainer opened an assignment and read the line under the target box: "Standard for this cell: 12." Their words: "it is not a standard, it is the target based on the standard. It is very misleading." Exactly right, and the confusion was one this feature introduced by shipping both quantities at once — the standard is the cycle time, seconds per unit, set once per cell and unmoving; the target is what that standard works out to for one window, and it changes with every resize and every efficiency edit. The pop-up hint now reads "Target from this cell's cycle time: 12" and the chip tooltip says "(from cycle time)" rather than "(standard)". The prop behind both was standardQty and is now derivedQty, since the name that misled the reader was misleading the code too. Two tests pin the wording because a string is what tsc cannot see. Nothing was computed wrongly and nothing was failing — the whole defect lived in what the screen called things, which is the kind only a person reading the words will find. Verified in the browser on the day the seeded assignments fall: a chip reading ⌖300 and the pop-up naming it a target from the cycle time. While driving it, one alarm turned out to be my own instrumentation rather than the product — a script that retyped 90 into a box which had reseeded to minutes, then to hours, leaving 90 hours behind; from a clean table one save writes exactly one correct row, checked three times. 1746 app tests in 56 files · tsc 0 · eslint 0 · commits eff559d

Session 37 · 2026-09-03 · A standard cycle time per cell per part, and the target the board derives from it (R-315..R-317) counts confirmed

The maintainer asked for a standard cycle time at each place work is actually booked, per part, so an assignment can say how much can be made in the time a person is there — "(how much time a person is assigned - breaks) / standard cycle time" — as a DEFAULT the existing target field still overrides. On their model for the level above, the sum, this agreed with a distinction: summing cells is right as labour content per unit, but it is not a line's output rate (sequential stations are limited by the slowest cell, parallel ones make more than either), so the roll-up is display only and never derives a target. Four commits. Migration 0040 stores the fact — seconds only, so a sum across cells cannot become a unit-conversion bug — and enforces WHERE it may live in the database rather than in the UI: the node's own level must be schedulable (read off the node, since 0014 made that index per template) and the part must be offered there by the same predicate the run and assignment guards ask. Both FKs cascade, which is load-bearing: delete_node deletes the two other per-node tables by name and cannot know about this one. board_window gains one key, scoped by the CTE already there. The derived target is computed, never stored, so a drag or a break edit is already right; it tiles breaks itself rather than reusing the board's breakInstances, which clips to the window and so cannot answer a block starting before it. A part slower than the shift shows one decimal instead of flooring to zero, at the maintainer's request. The chip and the direct block had the target reading duplicated line for line — the shape of the R-313 "units" bug — so it was extracted to one function before a third state was added to two copies. The admin grid is a new section: rows are the plant's tree, columns the parts it makes, entry in seconds/minutes/hours converted on the way in, and the display only offers a larger unit when the conversion is exact so re-saving an untouched row cannot change it. Nothing is mandatory anywhere; a blank cell is the ordinary state and its assignments simply read NA. Eleven SQL checks (64_cycle_times_test.sql) cover the two refusals, the CHECK, per-plant reading and writing, the RLS-filtered update that changes nothing and says nothing, the board payload, the cross-tenant FK and the cascade. JS suite 1715 green, up from 1663. Applied to the dev DB and round-tripped there as the real admin persona, including the client's exact upsert path. The verify-db.sh db_checks recount (+11) is owed on CI.

1715 app tests in 53 files · tsc 0 · eslint 0 · commits 8a81e25, bce8987, be4bb74, 11e6fc1

Session 36 · 2026-09-03 · The unit rule holds for old data too, enforced by a CHECK (R-314) counts confirmed

On the client-side units fix the maintainer said "a rule is a rule, be it new or old" — the old rows the previous code wrote still carried a unit with no quantity, and a code change does not reach back to rewrite stored rows. Migration 0039 makes the rule hold everywhere: a backfill nulls target_unit wherever target_qty is null, then a CHECK (target_unit IS NULL OR target_qty IS NOT NULL) refuses a unit without a quantity from any code path — the invariant becomes a fact of the schema, the twin of target_qty's check(>0), rather than something the UI remembers. Data-transforming, so it carries an upgrade check (upgrade_0039, 4 cases) run over a pre-0039 database: plant the dirty row, apply, assert the backfill, the refusal, and that a unit WITH a quantity still saves. Validated on a scratch DB built to 0038 (all four PASS) and applied to the dev DB — one dirty row backfilled to zero, a bad write refused. No column or RPC changed, so db:types is unaffected; JS suite 1663 green. The canonical verify-db.sh db_checks recount (+4) is owed on CI.
