1653 app tests in 48 files · tsc 0 · eslint 0 · commits 1f64248

Session 33 · 2026-09-03 · The training matrix header groups by plant (R-312) counts confirmed

The maintainer showed the training matrix with four plants: one plant split across two header groups, the plant order jumbled, and an Area 2 column floating with no plant above it. buildColumns trims every column to one common root via lowestCommonAncestor — fine for a single tree, wrong here, because a plant IS a root (parentId null) and there is no company node above the plants, so a multi-plant matrix has no shared ancestor. The old fallback chose an arbitrary owner as the root, pathFromRoot cut every chain at it (the training owned there lost its plant), and the sort, tying on root sortOrder, dropped to the training name and interleaved the plants. Fix: lowestCommonAncestor returns "" when nothing is common (in no chain, so no path is cut and each column keeps its full plant→owner ancestry), and the sort tiebreaks by node name at each level before the training name, keeping plants/areas/lines contiguous. Verified in the app as the company admin — the header now reads Plant A, Plant D, Plant B, Plant C, each once, with Area 2 under Plant D; screenshot sent. Also confirmed the wide table scrolls horizontally within its own .scroll container (overflow-x: auto), so ten plants get a scrollbar rather than overflowing the page. A new test builds plants as true roots and pins the grouping; the single-plant and company-root cases are unchanged. Suite 1653 green. tsc and eslint clean. No migration.

1653 app tests in 48 files · tsc 0 · eslint 0 · commits b46565b

Session 32 · 2026-09-03 · Both fixes actually landed — operator pool and one shared toggle, verified in the app counts confirmed

The maintainer ran session 31's work and neither fix showed: the board still listed every plant's operators, and the two toggles still looked different. Both were real misses. The operator filter resolved each operator's owner PATH through index.nodeById and failed OPEN when it could not find one — but index.nodeById holds only the SCOPED nodes, so a different plant's owner was never in it and every out-of-plant operator was kept: the opposite of the intent. The fix is membership — board_window's nodes ARE the selected plant's subtree, so an operator belongs iff index.nodeById.has(site_node_id); the path-based ownedUnder helper and its (passing, misleading) unit tests are removed. The toggle change had unified only the chevron glyph, leaving the button chrome as two separate stylesheets; PanelToggle now owns the button and both left panels render it. Both were VERIFIED by driving the running app as the company admin (Playwright): with Plant B selected the OPERATORS panel lists only Operator B1-B6, and the admin rail toggle and board OPERATORS toggle render as the same bordered chevron. Screenshots sent to the maintainer. Lesson recorded on R-310: the earlier helper passed its unit tests and still shipped the bug, because the scoped-map subtlety only appears against real data — so this one is proven in the app, not just in vitest. Suite 1652 green (was 1653; the five path-based ownedUnder cases were replaced by four membership ownedInScope cases). tsc and eslint clean. No migration.

Session 31 · 2026-09-03 · Board operator pool follows the plant, and one audited chevron (R-310, R-311) counts confirmed

Two things the maintainer raised. FIRST: a system admin picking a plant on the board still saw every operator in the company in the left panel. board_window returns all operators on purpose — S18 (52_scope_and_colour) pins the read wide so a cross-plant assignment can still be DRAWN, and D113 keeps an out-of-area person in the list, annotated, because cross-placement with a reason is a feature. Session 28 · 2026-09-03 · A system admin sets one date format for the whole company (R-309) counts confirmed

The maintainer asked for a Settings tab where a system admin sets the date format for the site, so dates read the same way throughout the app (Operators, Trainings, Import were named) while the data stays stored in its native form. Talked through, two decisions settled up front: org-wide (set by the system admin), not per-site -- a plant is only a root node, and a per-site format would need the node-scoped resolution per-site timezone (D88) reserves for Phase 2; and format now, timezone deferred to R-D88 -- the named surfaces show Postgres date columns, timezone-less by construction, so a timezone toggle would change nothing there. Then the maintainer asked to make sure any date shown in future adopts the format automatically, which became the guardrail below. Migration 0037 adds set_org_date_format, an RPC because a plain UPDATE on orgs.settings is a silent zero-row no-op for a non-admin under orgs_update: it pre-checks app_is_admin, validates a closed token enum (d_mon_yyyy default / dmy_slash / mdy_slash / iso), and merges the one key so capacity_cap and the rest survive. Rendering goes through one pure seam, src/lib/format/dates.ts (formatCalendarDay) -- no Date, no Intl, no timezone, because parsing a date through new Date moves it a day west of Greenwich; operators.ts stays dependency-free and keeps its own DEFAULT renderer for the reason strings it builds, with a test pinning the two defaults equal. useOrgSettings/useDateFormat carry the token to the components (gated on canQueryAsUser, D91), and a new system-admin-only Settings section (filtered on profile.role, since adminSectionsFor returns "all" for a site admin too) offers the four formats with a live sample. The guardrail is dateSeam.test.ts, a file audit in scaleAudit's shape: the build fails if a date is formatted with Intl.DateTimeFormat, toLocaleDateString/toLocaleTimeString or a hand-rolled month array anywhere but the two allowlisted seams -- so new code must go through the seam, which already reads the org format. App suite 1635 green (was 1615: dateFormat and dateSeam). tsc and eslint clean. The 6 SQL cases in 63_org_date_format_test.sql were run green against the local container (harness + migrations + seed) and db:types regenerated (one added line, the RPC -- the committed types were in sync); the canonical verify-db.sh db_checks recount is owed on CI, so the baseline db_checks is left at 522.

Session 27 · 2026-09-02 · A site admin makes, and wholly owns, a product at their own plant (D116) counts confirmed

The maintainer reopened the D115 Split on products: "a product is going to have unique part number, there is little to no risk to let a site admin add a new product." Reservation raised and taken — create is the birth of a company-wide identity, so letting it cross alone would let a site admin mint a part they could not then fix or remove. The maintainer chose the fuller shape (D116): a site admin creates AT a plant they administer, assigned in one act, and owns the whole lifecycle (rename, recolour, delete) WHILE only their plant makes it; a second plant adopting it hands the identity back to the company. Migration 0036 adds app_can_edit_product_record (the shared "do I administer every maker" predicate), widens the products UPDATE/DELETE policies and the delete_owned_row product branch onto it, and adds the create_product_at_node RPC (create + assign in one transaction, gated on app_is_admin_for(node), because an INSERT WITH CHECK cannot see the plant and app_is_admin_anywhere is visibility-only).
